Um, no... I don't really think of them like that. I wouldn't call them unwelcome, no. It's more like... well, they're just part of being a careful person, I think?

Well, um... I guess that's the hard part. I mean, I've always prided myself on being a good guy, you know? Someone who protects people. But... 

I guess I should just tell you why I'm really here. It's... it's just really hard to say out loud. I try to be a good man, I really do. But... I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts about being a danger to others pop into your mind unexpectedly?\n\nPatient: Well, um... not really. I mean, they don't just pop up out of nowhere when I'm not thinking about work or anything. It's usually when I'm on a job site, or fixing something around the house, that I start thinking about it. \n\nI hope that's what you meant? I just... I don't want you to think I'm losing my mind and having these random thoughts all day long. It's just when I'm dealing with wires and stuff, it makes sense that I'd think about it, right? I try to be really careful to answer your questions exactly how you need me to.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself thinking about being a danger to others when you're trying to focus on something else?\n\nPatient: No, no, I wouldn't say that. Does that sound okay to you?\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts about being a danger to others cause you a lot of distress or anxiety?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I wouldn't say that. Well, um... they definitely go against how I see myself. Or, how I want to see myself, I guess. I've always tried to be the guy who fixes things, the one who makes things safe. 

But, like I was saying... I just can't shake this thought. It's the whole reason I came in, really. I just keep thinking that if all my years as an electrician couldn't keep my own brother safe... then I must be a danger to anybody who works with me, or... or even the people living under my roof. 

It feels awful to say that out loud. I don't want to be that guy. I want to be a good man, a safe man. I hope you can see that I'm trying, right? I just... I don't want you to think this is who I really am deep down. Does that make sense?

I don't want you to think I'm losing my mind and having these random thoughts all day long. It's just when I'm dealing with wires and stuff, it makes sense that I'd think about it, right? Um... no, not really. I wouldn't say they force their way in. Like I was mentioning earlier, I kind of feel like I *need* to think about those things, you know? To make sure everything is safe.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi, Doctor. Thank you for seeing me. I, um... I hope I'm in the right place for this. I just keep thinking I'm a danger to anybody who works with me, or even the people living under my roof. Does that make sense? I just want to make sure I'm not wasting your time with all this.\n\nDoctor: Are these thoughts about being a danger to others something that you try to push away or avoid thinking about?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I don't think so. I definitely don't want to sound like I'm obsessing over it or anything bad like that, I just... I don't actively try to block the thoughts out. Am I explaining that right? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts about being a danger to others pop into your mind unexpectedly?\n\nPatient: Well, um... not really. I mean, they don't just pop up out of nowhere when I'm not thinking about work or anything. It's usually when I'm on a job site, or fixing something around the house, that I start thinking about it. \n\nI hope that's what you meant? I just... I don't want you to think I'm losing my mind and having these random thoughts all day long. It's just when I'm dealing with wires and stuff, it makes sense that I'd think about it, right? I try to be really careful to answer your questions exactly how you need me to.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself thinking about being a danger to others when you're trying to focus on something else?\n\nPatient: No, no, I wouldn't say that. If I'm, you know, watching a game or helping my daughter with her homework, I'm focused on that. The thoughts don't really interrupt me when I'm busy with other things. \n\nI just... I really try to stay present, especially for my family. I hope that's a good thing? I don't want you to think I'm completely consumed by this, because I'm not. I can still focus on normal stuff. Does that sound okay to you?\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts about being a danger to others cause you a lot of distress or anxiety?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I wouldn't say that. I don't really get anxious or distressed about it. It's more just... a sense of responsibility, I guess? Like, just being thorough with my work and making sure things are safe. \n\nIt doesn't upset me or make me panic or anything like that. I really hope that's a normal way to feel about it? I just want to make sure you know I'm handling things okay and not, you know, falling apart over it. I'm doing my best to keep it in perspective. Does that make sense to you?\n\nDoctor: Are these thoughts about being a danger to others affecting your ability to do your job as an electrician or your daily activities?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I mean, I try really hard not to let it affect anything. I'm still showing up every day, you know? But... I guess things have been a little rocky lately. \n\nI don't want to make a big deal out of it, but... some of the apprentices have been asking to work with other guys. And, well, I was actually pulled off this one solar job recently. There was a... a bit of a disagreement, and they said they didn't feel I was safe to have on the site. \n\nI just... I want you to know I'm a good electrician. I've been doing this a long time. It's just a rough patch, right? I'm sure I can get back on track if I just try harder. I hope you don't think less of me for that... everyone has off days at work, don't they?\n\nDoctor: Have these thoughts about being a danger to others affected your relationships with your family or friends?\n\nPatient: Oh, no. No, definitely not. My family is... they're everything to me. I make sure to leave all that stuff at the door when I come home, you know? My wife and my daughter, we're doing great. I'd never let anything get in the way of being a good husband and dad to them. \n\nAnd my buddies, we still hang out, watch the games, everything is totally normal there. I really try to be the guy they can all rely on, no matter what. I mean, that's what you're supposed to do, right? Keep it together for the people you care about? I really hope that shows I'm doing okay at home, at least. Does that sound like I'm handling that part right?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about being a danger to others are unwanted or unwelcome when they come up?\n\nPatient: Um, no... I don't really think of them like that. I wouldn't call them unwelcome, no. It's more like... well, they're just part of being a careful person, I think? \n\nI actually kind of want to be on top of things, so if a thought pops up about a hazard or making sure I'm not putting anyone at risk, I just see it as doing my due diligence. That's a good trait to have, right? Being cautious? I'd rather be safe than sorry, so I don't really mind them. I mean, I hope it's okay that I feel that way... I just want to be a responsible guy, and I figured that's how you're supposed to handle it. Does that sound right to you?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about being a danger to others are in line with your own values and beliefs about yourself?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess that's the hard part. I mean, I've always prided myself on being a good guy, you know? Someone who protects people. But... \n\nI guess I should just tell you why I'm really here. It's... it's just really hard to say out loud. I try to be a good man, I really do. But... I just can't shake this thought that... well, if all my years of experience as an electrician couldn't keep my own brother safe... then how can I be safe around anyone else? \n\nIt just feels like I'm a danger to anybody who works with me, or even... even the people living under my own roof. I don't want to believe that about myself, I really don't. I want to be the guy who fixes things, not the guy who hurts people. That makes sense, right? That I'd struggle with that? I just... I hope you don't think I'm a terrible person for having those thoughts.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about being a danger to others are a part of who you are, or do they go against how you see yourself?\n\nPatient: Well, um... they definitely go against how I see myself. Or, how I want to see myself, I guess. I've always tried to be the guy who fixes things, the one who makes things safe. \n\nBut, like I was saying... I just can't shake this thought. It's the whole reason I came in, really. I just keep thinking that if all my years as an electrician couldn't keep my own brother safe... then I must be a danger to anybody who works with me, or... or even the people living under my roof. \n\nIt feels awful to say that out loud. I don't want to be that guy. I want to be a good man, a safe man. I hope you can see that I'm trying, right? I just... I don't want you to think this is who I really am deep down. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts about being a danger to others ever feel like they are forcing their way into your mind, even when you don't want to think about them?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I wouldn't say they force their way in. Like I was mentioning earlier, I kind of feel like I *need* to think about those things, you know? To make sure everything is safe. \n\nSo it's not like they're just invading my brain out of nowhere when I don't want them there... it's more like I'm actively trying to stay on high alert so I don't make another mistake. I mean, if I don't think about all the risks and how I might mess up, who will? I just want to be responsible and make sure nobody else gets hurt on my watch. That's a normal way to handle it, right? I'm just trying to do the right thing... I hope that's what I'm supposed to be doing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I guess maybe a little bit? I try to stay active and be helpful where I can, but... yeah, I suppose things have changed. Like, I used to volunteer to help with the electrical stuff at my church, but I quit doing that recently. I told them I was just taking a break, but honestly, I just couldn't bring myself to do it. I hope it's okay that I stepped back from that... I just didn't want to risk anyone's safety. 

And, um... I don't really go into my own garage anymore, either, unless I absolutely have to. It's just... easier that way. Plus, if anyone starts talking about car projects, or... or the fire... I shut them down pretty fast. I know it's probably rude of me to cut people off like that, and I really hate being impolite, but I just can't handle talking about it. That makes sense, doesn't it? That I'd just want to avoid those kinds of things for now?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... no, not really. I don't do anything like that. I mean, I check things, sure, like making sure the stove is off or the doors are locked, but... that's just normal, right? Just being responsible. 

I wouldn't call it a ritual or anything weird like that. I just want to make sure I'm doing my part to keep things safe. I don't have to flip a light switch a certain number of times or... or wash my hands over and over. Nothing like that. I just try to be careful. That's a good thing, isn't it? I just want you to know I'm not doing anything strange.

I guess things have been a little rocky lately. \n\nI don't want to make a big deal out of it, but... some of the apprentices have been asking to work with other guys. And, well, I was actually pulled off this one solar job recently. There was a... a bit of a disagreement, and they said they didn't feel I was safe to have on the site. \n\nI just... I want you to know I'm a good electrician. I've been doing this a long time. It's just a rough patch, right? I'm sure I can get back on track if I just try harder. I hope you don't think less of me for that... everyone has off days at work, don't they?\n\nDoctor: Have these thoughts about being a danger to others affected your relationships with your family or friends?\n\nPatient: Oh, no. No, definitely not. My family is... they're everything to me. I make sure to leave all that stuff at the door when I come home, you know? My wife and my daughter, we're doing great. I'd never let anything get in the way of being a good husband and dad to them. \n\nAnd my buddies, we still hang out, watch the games, everything is totally normal there. I really try to be the guy they can all rely on, no matter what. I mean, that's what you're supposed to do, right? Keep it together for the people you care about? I really hope that shows I'm doing okay at home, at least. Does that sound like I'm handling that part right?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about being a danger to others are unwanted or unwelcome when they come up?\n\nPatient: Um, no... I don't really think of them like that. I wouldn't call them unwelcome, no. It's more like... well, they're just part of being a careful person, I think? \n\nI actually kind of want to be on top of things, so if a thought pops up about a hazard or making sure I'm not putting anyone at risk, I just see it as doing my due diligence. That's a good trait to have, right? Being cautious? I'd rather be safe than sorry, so I don't really mind them. I mean, I hope it's okay that I feel that way... I just want to be a responsible guy, and I figured that's how you're supposed to handle it. Does that sound right to you?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about being a danger to others are in line with your own values and beliefs about yourself?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess that's the hard part. I mean, I've always prided myself on being a good guy, you know? Someone who protects people. But... \n\nI guess I should just tell you why I'm really here. It's... it's just really hard to say out loud. I try to be a good man, I really do. But... I just can't shake this thought that... well, if all my years of experience as an electrician couldn't keep my own brother safe... then how can I be safe around anyone else? \n\nIt just feels like I'm a danger to anybody who works with me, or even... even the people living under my own roof. I don't want to believe that about myself, I really don't. I want to be the guy who fixes things, not the guy who hurts people. That makes sense, right? That I'd struggle with that? I just... I hope you don't think I'm a terrible person for having those thoughts.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about being a danger to others are a part of who you are, or do they go against how you see yourself?\n\nPatient: Well, um... they definitely go against how I see myself.   }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I wouldn't say it's causing a *lot* of pain. I mean, I try to keep a positive attitude and just push through, you know? But... I guess, if I'm being completely honest—and I want to be honest with you—it has caused a little bit of a bump in the road lately. Just at work. 

I'm an electrician, and I've always prided myself on being really good at what I do. But... well, I guess my work has started slipping a little bit. Not because I don't know what I'm doing! Just... maybe I've been a little too intense about making sure everything is secure. It's actually gotten to the point where the apprentices won't pair with me right now. I think I just make them a little nervous because I'm so focused on everything. 

And, um... there was an incident. I was on this big solar job recently, and... there was a confrontation. It was just a misunderstanding, really, I swear. I was just trying to make sure things were being handled right, but they ended up pulling me off the job. They told me... they said I wasn't safe to have on site. 

It was really embarrassing. I didn't mean to cause any trouble, I was just trying to look out for everyone. You understand how that can happen, right? When you're just trying to do a good job and it gets taken the wrong way? I just want to get back to being a reliable guy for them. I'm not a bad person or anything like that, I promise.

I really hope that shows I'm doing okay at home, at least. I don't really think of them like that. I wouldn't call them unwelcome, no. That's a good trait to have, right? Being cautious? I'd rather be safe than sorry, so I don't really mind them. I mean, I hope it's okay that I feel that way... I just want to be a responsible guy, and I figured that's how you're supposed to handle it. Does that sound right to you?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about being a danger to others are in line with your own values and beliefs about yourself?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess that's the hard part. I mean, I've always prided myself on being a good guy, you know? Someone who protects people. But... \n\nI guess I should just tell you why I'm really here. It's... it's just really hard to say out loud. I try to be a good man, I really do. But... I just can't shake this thought that... well, if all my years of experience as an electrician couldn't keep my own brother safe... then how can I be safe around anyone else? \n\nIt just feels like I'm a danger to anybody who works with me, or even... even the people living under my own roof. I don't want to believe that about myself, I really don't. I want to be the guy who fixes things, not the guy who hurts people. That makes sense, right? That I'd struggle with that? I just... It's the whole reason I came in, really. I just keep thinking that if all my years as an electrician couldn't keep my own brother safe... then I must be a danger to anybody who works with me, or... or even the people living under my roof. \n\nIt feels awful to say that out loud. I don't want to be that guy. I want to be a good man, a safe man. I hope you can see that I'm trying, right? I just... I don't want you to think this is who I really am deep down. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts about being a danger to others ever feel like they are forcing their way into your mind, even when you don't want to think about them?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I wouldn't say they force their way in. Like I was mentioning earlier, I kind of feel like I *need* to think about those things, you know? To make sure everything is safe. \n\nSo it's not like they're just invading my brain out of nowhere when I don't want them there... it's more like I'm actively trying to stay on high alert so I don't make another mistake. I mean, if I don't think about all the risks and how I might mess up, who will? I just want to be responsible and make sure nobody else gets hurt on my watch. That's a normal way to handle it, right? I'm just trying to do the right thing... I hope that's what I'm supposed to be doing.\n\nDoctor: Are these thoughts affecting your hobbies or leisure activities in any way?\n\nPatient: Well, um... Well, um... I try really hard not to let it change me. I always want to be the same reliable, good guy I've always been, you know? 

But... if I'm being completely honest with you—and I know I need to be—this is actually the main reason I finally came in to talk to someone. I'm just... I'm a little ashamed to admit it, I guess. I really hope you don't judge me or think poorly of me for this. 

It's just... I can't shake this one thought. I've been doing this a long time, right? I know the trade inside and out. But... if all my years of experience as an electrician couldn't keep my own brother safe... then how can I trust myself? 

It just makes me feel like... like maybe I really am a danger to anybody who works with me. Or even the people living under my roof. I don't *want* to be that kind of person, I really don't. I'm just so terrified of letting anyone else down or causing someone else to get hurt. You can understand why I'd feel that way, right? It makes sense to question yourself after something like that happens, doesn't it? I just hope I'm making sense to you.

That's a good trait to have, right? Being cautious? I'd rather be safe than sorry, so I don't really mind them. I mean, I hope it's okay that I feel that way... I just want to be a responsible guy, and I figured that's how you're supposed to handle it. Does that sound right to you?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about being a danger to others are in line with your own values and beliefs about yourself?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess that's the hard part. I mean, I've always prided myself on being a good guy, you know? Someone who protects people. But... \n\nI guess I should just tell you why I'm really here. It's... it's just really hard to say out loud. I try to be a good man, I really do. But... It's the whole reason I came in, really. I just keep thinking that if all my years as an electrician couldn't keep my own brother safe... then I must be a danger to anybody who works with me, or... or even the people living under my roof. \n\nIt feels awful to say that out loud. I don't want to be that guy. I want to be a good man, a safe man. I hope you can see that I'm trying, right? I just... I don't want you to think this is who I really am deep down. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts about being a danger to others ever feel like they are forcing their way into your mind, even when you don't want to think about them?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I wouldn't say they force their way in. Like I was mentioning earlier, I kind of feel like I *need* to think about those things, you know? To make sure everything is safe. \n\nSo it's not like they're just invading my brain out of nowhere when I don't want them there... it's more like I'm actively trying to stay on high alert so I don't make another mistake. I mean, if I don't think about all the risks and how I might mess up, who will? I just want to be responsible and make sure nobody else gets hurt on my watch. That's a normal way to handle it, right? I'm just trying to do the right thing... I hope that's what I'm supposed to be doing.\n\nDoctor: Are these thoughts affecting your hobbies or leisure activities in any way?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess maybe a little bit? I try to stay active and be helpful where I can, but... yeah, I suppose things have changed. Like, I used to volunteer to help with the electrical stuff at my church, but I quit doing that recently. I told them I was just taking a break, but honestly, I just couldn't bring myself to do it. I hope it's okay that I stepped back from that... I just didn't want to risk anyone's safety. \n\nAnd, um... I don't really go into my own garage anymore, either, unless I absolutely have to. It's just... easier that way. Plus, if anyone starts talking about car projects, or... or the fire... I shut them down pretty fast. I know it's probably rude of me to cut people off like that, and I really hate being impolite, but I just can't handle talking about it. That makes sense, doesn't it? That I'd just want to avoid those kinds of things for now?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to perform any specific rituals or behaviors to reduce your concerns about being a danger to others?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I don't do anything like that. I mean, I check things, sure, like making sure the stove is off or the doors are locked, but... that's just normal, right? Just being responsible. \n\nI wouldn't call it a ritual or anything weird like that. I just want to make sure I'm doing my part to keep things safe. I don't have to flip a light switch a certain number of times or... or wash my hands over and over. Nothing like that. I just try to be careful. That's a good thing, isn't it? I just want you to know I'm not doing anything strange.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts about being a danger to others that you didn't intentionally think about and wish you hadn't had?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, I don't think so. Like I said before, the thoughts I have about safety and all that... they're things I feel like I *should* be thinking about. It's not like they just pop into my head against my will and make me upset that they're there. \n\nI'm usually the one bringing them up in my own mind, just trying to stay ahead of things. I don't have any of those weird, unwanted thoughts that just sneak up on you. It's all just me trying to be careful and responsible. That's how it's supposed to be, right? I'm just trying to make sure I'm doing the right thing. I hope that's the kind of answer you're looking for... I really don't have anything like what you're describing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about being a danger to others are something you'd rather not think about, but can't help thinking about anyway?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say I *can't* help it. I mean, they are thoughts I'd rather not dwell on, sure, because nobody wants to think about bad things happening, right? But it's not like I'm losing control of my own mind or anything. \n\nIt's more just... I'm choosing to think about them because I need to be responsible. I want to make sure I'm doing the right thing and keeping everyone safe. I don't feel like I'm trapped by the thoughts or anything like that. It's just me trying to be a good, careful person. That's a normal way to look at it, don't you think? I just want to make sure I'm explaining it right so you don't think I'm, you know, going crazy or something.\n\nDoctor: Are these thoughts about being a danger to others significantly interfering with your daily life or causing you a lot of emotional pain?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say it's causing a *lot* of pain. I mean, I try to keep a positive attitude and just push through, you know? But... I guess, if I'm being completely honest—and I want to be honest with you—it has caused a little bit of a bump in the road lately. Just at work. \n\nI'm an electrician, and I've always prided myself on being really good at what I do. But... well, I guess my work has started slipping a little bit. Not because I don't know what I'm doing! Just... maybe I've been a little too intense about making sure everything is secure. It's actually gotten to the point where the apprentices won't pair with me right now. I think I just make them a little nervous because I'm so focused on everything. \n\nAnd, um... there was an incident. I was on this big solar job recently, and... there was a confrontation. It was just a misunderstanding, really, I swear. I was just trying to make sure things were being handled right, but they ended up pulling me off the job. They told me... they said I wasn't safe to have on site. \n\nIt was really embarrassing. I didn't mean to cause any trouble, I was just trying to look out for everyone. You understand how that can happen, right? When you're just trying to do a good job and it gets taken the wrong way? I just want to get back to being a reliable guy for them. I'm not a bad person or anything like that, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel an overwhelming urge to repeat certain actions or behaviors to reduce your concerns about being a danger to others?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, I don't feel any kind of... overwhelming urge like that. I'm not doing things over and over again like some kind of compulsion, if that's what you mean. \n\nLike I said, I just try to be careful. If I double-check a wire or make sure a panel is secure, it's just because I'm being thorough. It's not because I have some uncontrollable urge to do it. I'm just doing my job the right way, you know? Taking pride in my work and making sure nobody gets hurt. \n\nI definitely don't have any weird habits or anything where I'm repeating things for no reason. I'm in control of what I'm doing. That makes sense, doesn't it? I just want to be sure I'm giving you the right picture here. I'm just a normal guy trying to be responsible.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about being a danger to others are changing the way you see yourself as a person?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I try really hard not to let it change me. I always want to be the same reliable, good guy I've always been, you know? \n\nBut... if I'm being completely honest with you—and I know I need to be—this is actually the main reason I finally came in to talk to someone. I'm just... I'm a little ashamed to admit it, I guess. I really hope you don't judge me or think poorly of me for this. \n\nIt's just... I can't shake this one thought.
